# Unit Prices v2.31.0 Implementation Plan — column names, OpenAI official price, prompt caching, GPT long context

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Rename the `/pricing` columns to "AWS Bedrock - Global CRIS / US CRIS / In Region", order the tables Anthropic Claude → OpenAI → Amazon Nova, replace the OpenAI table's Claude Platform on AWS column with OpenAI's official price list, and show prompt-caching prices (every channel) and GPT long-context prices, all synced from official sources every 12 hours.

**Architecture:** `price_history` rows gain seven nullable price columns (cache read, cache write, 1h cache write, long-context input/output/cache read/cache write). Parsers read them from the same official sources (agreement offers, Price List, Anthropic doc) plus a fourth source, the OpenAI pricing markdown, which feeds a new display-only `openai_list` channel stored under synthetic model ids `openai-list:<family_key>`. The new fields are display-only: cost pages keep using input/output. First-time values fill existing rows in place (`enriched`), value changes follow the existing 50% gate per field.

**Tech Stack:** FastAPI + SQLAlchemy 2.0 (psycopg2, pinned `<2.1`), PostgreSQL 16 / SQLite in tests, Next.js 16 + React + Tailwind, vitest, Playwright (chromium).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-pricing-v2-31-design.md` (base design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-pricing-menu-design.md`, ADR-030). Where this plan and the spec differ, this plan's Interface Contract wins (it was re-derived from the 2026-09-27 source data). Task 11 Step 3 appends the list of those differences to the end of the spec (the spec body stays as the decision record), so the committed design and the implementation agree.

## Global Constraints

- Version: **v2.31.0** (bumped in Task 11 only).
- Backend tests: `cd backend && python3.12 -m pytest -q …` (system python3.9 cannot run FastAPI `X | Y` hints). No network in tests: fake fetchers and the committed fixtures in `backend/tests/fixtures/pricing/` only.
- Never add `from __future__ import annotations` to FastAPI modules.
- Frontend: `cd frontend && npx vitest run …`, `npm run typecheck`. Never run `next dev`. e2e: `npm run build && PLAYWRIGHT_USE_PRODUCTION=1 CI=1 npx playwright test --project=chromium` (webkit cannot launch on this host). Revert incidental changes to `frontend/next-env.d.ts`, `frontend/CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`.
- e2e environment (both seen on this host):
  1. In a git worktree, install `frontend/node_modules` with `npm ci` (or a real copy), never a symlink to another checkout. With a symlink vitest and tsc pass but `npm run build` (Turbopack) fails with `Symlink [project]/node_modules is invalid, it points out of the filesystem root`.
  2. The Playwright web server is fixed to port 3100 and `CI=1` turns `reuseExistingServer` off, so a server already on 3100 (another checkout, a parallel reviewer) ends the run at once with `http://127.0.0.1:3100 is already used`. Check first with `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' http://127.0.0.1:3100` (`000` = free). When it is taken, run on a free port with a throwaway copy of the config **inside `frontend/`** (the config's `testDir`, its `@playwright/test` import and the web server's working directory all resolve from the config's directory, so a copy elsewhere finds no tests), and delete it afterwards: `cd frontend && sed 's/3100/3187/g' playwright.config.ts > playwright.port.config.ts && PLAYWRIGHT_USE_PRODUCTION=1 CI=1 npx playwright test -c playwright.port.config.ts --project=chromium …; rm -f playwright.port.config.ts`. Check the new port the same way first (any free port works). Never reuse the other server and never commit the copy.
- Push: the branch is pushed only in Task 12. Tasks 8 and 9 leave `frontend/e2e/pricing.spec.ts` red until Task 10 rewrites it, and CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) runs `npm run test:e2e` on every pull-request push, so Tasks 1–11 commit locally only.
- Never stage `tests/20260810_SB/` (untracked, public repo). Never write offerToken, presigned URLs or AWS credentials into code, fixtures or logs.
- Korean UI and export copy: commas instead of middle dots, numbered lists "1.", assertive wording. Copy strings in this plan are final — use them verbatim.
- `FAMILY_ORDER` (backend `pricing_sources.py` = frontend `sortModels.ts`) does not change.
- Prices are USD per 1M tokens. Stored as float; compared and stored at 6 decimals (`PRICE_QUANTUM`).
- The fixtures in `backend/tests/fixtures/pricing/` were regenerated from the 2026-09-27 official sources before this plan (commit "test(pricing): regenerate fixtures from 2026-09-27 sources"): the offer rate cards now keep cache and long-context dimensions, 4 new offer fixtures (`offers_claude-fable-5-1.json`, `offers_gpt-5.5.json`, `offers_gpt-5.6-terra.json`, `offers_gpt-5.6-luna.json`), Nova gains cache read/write items, `openai_pricing.md` is new. Do not hand-edit fixtures.
- Commit after each task with the message the task gives; end commit bodies with the repository's usual trailer if one is configured.

## Interface Contract

Every task implicitly includes this section. Names, signatures, field names and copy are binding.

### C1. Price fields (`backend/pricing_parsers.py`)

```python
EXTRA_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "cache_read", "cache_write", "cache_write_1h",
    "long_input", "long_output", "long_cache_read", "long_cache_write",
)
PRICE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = ("input", "output", *EXTRA_FIELDS)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class UnitPrice:
    input: Decimal
    output: Decimal
    cache_read: Decimal | None = None       # cache hit / cached input
    cache_write: Decimal | None = None      # Claude 5-minute write, OpenAI "cache writes", Nova cache write
    cache_write_1h: Decimal | None = None   # Claude 1-hour write
    long_input: Decimal | None = None       # GPT long context (above OpenAI's short-context limit)
    long_output: Decimal | None = None
    long_cache_read: Decimal | None = None
    long_cache_write: Decimal | None = None
```

`price_history` column for field `f` is `f"{f}_per_mtok"` (existing `input_per_mtok`, `output_per_mtok`; new `cache_read_per_mtok`, `cache_write_per_mtok`, `cache_write_1h_per_mtok`, `long_input_per_mtok`, `long_output_per_mtok`, `long_cache_read_per_mtok`, `long_cache_write_per_mtok`, all `Column(Float, nullable=True)`).

Zero: input/output must be > 0 (unchanged). Extra fields may be exactly 0 (Nova cache write is officially $0.00); negative is never accepted.

### C2. Agreement offer dimensions

Allow-list (full match), replacing `DIMENSION_RE`:

```python
DIMENSION_RE = re.compile(
    r"^(?:(?P<rc>APN2|USE1|USE2|USW2)_)?"
    r"(?:(?P<new>input_tokens|output_tokens|cache_read_tokens|cached_input_tokens"
    r"|cache_write_tokens|cache_write_tokens_1h|cache_write_tokens_30m)(?P<long>_long_ctx)?(?P<g>_global)?_standard"
    r"|(?P<legacy>InputTokenCount|OutputTokenCount|CacheReadInputTokenCount|CacheWriteInputTokenCount"
    r"|CacheWrite1hInputTokenCount)(?P<G>_Global)?)$"
)
```

Not matched (stay excluded): batch/flex/priority/fast, `_LCtx` (Claude legacy long context equals the base price), `cache_writes_tokens*` and every other prefix (EU_, UGE1_, Reserved…).

Name → field (priority 1 wins over 2 within one candidate key):

| new-scheme name | without `_long_ctx` | with `_long_ctx` | priority |
|---|---|---|---|
| `input_tokens` | `input` | `long_input` | 1 |
| `output_tokens` | `output` | `long_output` | 1 |
| `cache_read_tokens` | `cache_read` | `long_cache_read` | 1 |
| `cached_input_tokens` | `cache_read` | `long_cache_read` | 2 |
| `cache_write_tokens` | `cache_write` | `long_cache_write` | 1 |
| `cache_write_tokens_30m` | `cache_write` | `long_cache_write` | 1 |
| `cache_write_tokens_1h` | `cache_write_1h` | (ignored) | 1 |

| legacy name | field |
|---|---|
| `InputTokenCount` | `input` |
| `OutputTokenCount` | `output` |
| `CacheReadInputTokenCount` | `cache_read` |
| `CacheWriteInputTokenCount` | `cache_write` |
| `CacheWrite1hInputTokenCount` | `cache_write_1h` |

Why priority: GPT 5.6 offers carry both `cache_read_tokens` (current, equals the OpenAI doc: Terra Global 0.2) and `cached_input_tokens` (pre-price-cut value, Terra Global 0.25); GPT 5.4/5.5 `APN2_` carries only `cached_input_tokens`. `cache_writes_tokens` (Terra 3.125) is the pre-cut write and is never read.

Candidate keys stay `(scheme, region code or "", global?)` with the existing per-channel order (`_GLOBAL_ORDER`, `_US_ORDER`, in-region `(new, <code>, False), (new, "", False)`). Only entries with `unit == "Units"` and price > 0 count. Within one key, the same `(field, priority)` seen twice with different prices is ambiguous and dropped (existing rule, now per field). `select_offer_price(rate_card, channel)`: the **first key that has both `input` and `output`** wins; every extra field comes from **that same key** (lowest priority number available), missing ones are `None`.

### C3. Price List (Nova)

`pricing_sources.NOVA_CACHE_USAGETYPES: dict[str, tuple[str, str]] = {"nova-2-lite": ("USE1-Nova2.0Lite-cache-read-input-token-count", "USE1-Nova2.0Lite-cache-write-input-token-count")}` (read, write). `NOVA_USAGETYPES` is unchanged.

```python
def parse_pricelist(price_list: list, input_usagetype: str, output_usagetype: str,
                    cache_read_usagetype: str | None = None, cache_write_usagetype: str | None = None) -> UnitPrice:
```

Input/output keep today's strict rules (exactly one product, one OnDemand dimension, unit "1K tokens", USD > 0, else `PriceParseError`). Each cache usagetype is optional and fail-soft: `None` usagetype, no product, more than one product or dimension, another unit, a missing/negative USD → that field is `None` (never an exception). USD 0 is accepted (0 × 1000 = 0). Fixture result: input 0.33, output 2.75, cache_read 0.0825, cache_write 0.

### C4. Anthropic pricing markdown (CP)

`parse_anthropic_pricing_md` keeps its required headers ("Model", "Base input tokens", "Output tokens") and adds optional headers (normalized with `_norm_header`): `"cache hits and refreshes"` → `cache_read`, `"5m cache writes"` → `cache_write`, `"1h cache writes"` → `cache_write_1h`. An extra value cell that is not exactly `$<n> / MTok` after `<sup>` removal is `None`. The ambiguity rule compares whole `UnitPrice` values. Fixture results: "Claude Fable 5.1" 10/50, 0.25, 12.5, 20; "Claude Opus 5.5" 4/20, 0.2, 5, 8; "Claude Sonnet 5" 2/10, 0.2, 2.5, 4; "Claude Haiku 4.5" 1/5, 0.1, 1.25, 2.

### C5. OpenAI pricing markdown (new source)

```python
def parse_openai_pricing_md(markdown: str) -> dict[str, UnitPrice]:
```

- Section: the first line equal (after `strip()`) to `### Standard pricing data`; missing → `PriceParseError("'### Standard pricing data' heading not found")`. Only the first table after it is read (the Batch/Flex/Fast tables below never are); a `#` heading before any table, or fewer than 3 table lines → `PriceParseError`.
- Headers by `_norm_header` name. Required: `"model"`, `"short context input"`, `"short context output"` (else `PriceParseError`). Optional: `"short context cached input"` → `cache_read`, `"short context cache writes"` → `cache_write`, `"long context input"` → `long_input`, `"long context output"` → `long_output`, `"long context cached input"` → `long_cache_read`, `"long context cache writes"` → `long_cache_write`.
- Value cell: exactly `^\$(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)$` → `Decimal`; anything else (`-`, blank) → `None`. A row without both short-context input and output is skipped.
- Model name: `_clean_model_name` (removes `<sup>` and the trailing parenthesis group: `gpt-5.5 (<272K context length)` → `gpt-5.5`). Lookups are exact (`gpt-5.4` ≠ `gpt-5.4-mini`). A name seen twice with different values is dropped. No parseable rows → `PriceParseError`.
- Fixture results: `gpt-6-astra` 10/50, cache_read 1, cache_write 12.5, long 20/75, 2, 25; `gpt-5.5` 5/30, 0.5, None, long 10/45, 1, None; `gpt-5.4` 2.5/15, 0.25, None, long 5/22.5, 0.5, None; `gpt-5.4-mini` 0.75/4.5 with every long field None; `gpt-5.6-sol` 4/20, 0.4, 5, long 8/30, 0.8, 10.

```python
PROVIDER_ORDER: tuple[str, ...] = ("anthropic", "openai", "amazon")
OPENAI_PRICING_URL = "https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing.md"
OPENAI_SOURCE_ID = "openai-pricing"
OPENAI_REFERENCE_URL = "https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing"
OPENAI_LIST_PREFIX = "openai-list:"

def openai_list_model_id(family_key: str) -> str:  # "openai-list:gpt-6-astra"
```

- `PriceIdentity.channel` gains `"openai_list"`; `source_kind` gains `"openai_doc"`.
- `price_identity("openai-list:<fk>")` → `PriceIdentity(fk, family, "openai", "openai_list", "openai_doc", fk)` when `fk` is one of the eight `_OPENAI_FM` family keys, else `None`.
- `tier_of("openai_list") == "openai_list"`; `region_of` unchanged.
- `active_channels(models, hidden)`: after today's loop, for each OpenAI family key present among the collected channels (provider `"openai"`, first-appearance order), append `openai_list_model_id(fk)` → its identity (skip when already present). Synthetic ids never appear in `AVAILABLE_MODELS` or `probe_results`.
- `PRICE_NOTES` (the only note) becomes:

```python
PRICE_NOTES: list[dict] = [{
    "family_key": "gpt-5.6-sol",
    "kind": "promo",
    "min_until": "2026-11-21",
    "prior_price": {"openai_list": {"input": 5, "output": 30}, "global": {"input": 5, "output": 30},
                    "in_region": {"input": 5.5, "output": 33}},
    "text_ko": "프로모션 단가다. OpenAI 공식 요금 문서에 최소 2026-11-21까지 적용한다고 기재돼 있다.",
    "text_en": "Promotional price. The OpenAI pricing page states that it applies at least through 2026-11-21.",
    "source": "openai_doc",
    "source_id": OPENAI_SOURCE_ID,
}]
```

  A note with `"source": "manual_note"` stays supported (it needs `basis_ko`/`basis_en`; its `source_id` is `note_source_id(family_key)`).

### C7. Storage and migration

- `models.PriceHistory` gets the seven C1 columns.
- `pricing_seed.ensure_price_columns(engine) -> list[str]`: inspect `price_history` columns (`sqlalchemy.inspect`); if none of the seven is missing return `[]` without executing any DDL (so no ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock is requested on every start). Otherwise, in one `engine.begin()` transaction: PostgreSQL runs `SET LOCAL statement_timeout = '30000'`, `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5000'`, then `ALTER TABLE price_history ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS <col> DOUBLE PRECISION` per missing column; other dialects run `ALTER TABLE price_history ADD COLUMN <col> FLOAT`. Returns the missing column names in `EXTRA_FIELDS` order. Exceptions propagate.
- Called from `main.py` lifespan in its own `try` block (log `"Price column migration failed (non-fatal, backend continues)"`) directly before the price seed block, and from `pricing_sync_runner.main` right after `create_tables()` (failure → log + `EXIT_FAILED`).

### C8. Seed (`backend/pricing_seed.py`)

- `SEED_SOURCE_DATE = date(2026, 9, 27)`.
- `SEED`, `CP_SEED` keep their `(input, output, source_id)` shape and values.
- New: `OPENAI_LIST_SEED: dict[str, tuple[float, float, str]]` (family_key → input, output, `OPENAI_SOURCE_ID`) and extras tables; one accessor:

```python
def seed_extra(model_id: str, ident: PriceIdentity) -> dict[str, float | None]:
    """All seven EXTRA_FIELDS for one active channel (None where the source has no such price)."""
```

- `seed_rows(active)` resolves `openai_list` channels through `OPENAI_LIST_SEED` (family_key) as it does CP through `CP_SEED`.
- `ensure_seed(engine, active) -> int` (return value unchanged: rows inserted): inserted rows carry the extras; in the same transaction and lock, existing `status='seed'` rows of the same model_ids whose input and output equal the seed values (rounded to 6 decimals) get each NULL extra column set to the seed value where the seed has one. Logs `"Price seed filled cache/long-context fields on %d rows"` when > 0.

Extras (USD per 1M; `None` = no such price). Bedrock Claude, Global = `APN2_*_global_standard` / legacy `APN2_*_Global`, US = `USE1_*_standard` / legacy `USE1_*`:

### C9. Sync (`backend/pricing_sync.py`)

```python
@dataclass
class Fetchers:
    offers: Callable[[str], dict]           # FM id -> list_foundation_model_agreement_offers response
    pricelist: Callable[..., list]          # (*usagetypes) -> PriceList items for all of them
    anthropic_doc: Callable[[], str]        # markdown text
    openai_doc: Callable[[], str]           # markdown text (OPENAI_PRICING_URL)

SOURCES = ("offers", "pricelist", "anthropic_doc", "openai_doc")
```

- `default_fetchers(...).pricelist(*usagetypes)` pages `get_products` for each usagetype in order and concatenates. `openai_doc()` GETs `OPENAI_PRICING_URL` with the same client, `User-Agent` and `_with_retries` as `anthropic_doc`.
- Fetch order: Anthropic doc → OpenAI doc → Price List → offers. The OpenAI doc block mirrors the Anthropic block (`parse_openai_pricing_md`, lookup by `ident.source_ref`, source id `OPENAI_SOURCE_ID`, errors `"openai_doc: …"`, `"openai_doc: model {name!r} not in the table"`). The Price List call passes `NOVA_USAGETYPES[fk] + NOVA_CACHE_USAGETYPES.get(fk, ())` and `parse_pricelist(items, in, out, *cache)`.
- Both doc blocks quantize **only the looked-up models** (a local `settle_doc` helper): the parsed table is kept raw, and each doc model name that an active channel needs is passed through `_quantized` on its own. A tracked model whose value fails `_quantized` skips only its own channels (`skipped:parse_failed`, error `"<source> <doc name>: <message>"`); an untracked row (the OpenAI Standard table has about 40 models) with a `$0.00` input or an extra that rounds to 0 never skips the tracked channels. A parser exception (missing heading or table) still skips all of that source's channels.
- `_quantized(price)`: every non-None field quantized to `PRICE_QUANTUM`; input/output ≤ 0 after rounding → `PriceParseError` (unchanged); an extra field that is negative, or positive but rounds to 0 → `PriceParseError`; 0 stays 0.
- Long-context prices are GPT-only: in the offers block, `_gpt_long_only(price, ident)` sets the four `long_*` fields to `None` for every channel whose `ident.provider != "openai"`, before `_quantized` (the C2 allow-list accepts `_long_ctx` names on any offer; the doc parsers and the Price List never produce `long_*` for Claude or Nova).
- Comparison:

```python
def classify_change(current: Mapping[str, float | None] | None, new: UnitPrice) -> str:
    """"no_baseline" | "unchanged" | "enriched" | "changed" | "pending" over PRICE_FIELDS."""
```

  `current` maps each of `PRICE_FIELDS` to the stored float or `None`. `None` → `"no_baseline"`. Per field (old via `_dec`, new from `new`): both `None` or old set / new `None` → ignored; old `None` / new set → *fill*; both set and different → *differs*, and *pending* when `old <= 0` or `|new − old| / old > 0.5`. Result: any *pending* → `"pending"`; else any *differs* → `"changed"`; else any *fill* → `"enriched"`; else `"unchanged"`.

- `_apply` (results as today plus `"enriched"`): *merged* values = the observed value where not `None`, else the current row's value.
  - `unchanged`: as today (observed_at, run_id, source_id on the current row).
  - `enriched`: as `unchanged`, plus each NULL extra column that the observation has is set on the current row. No new row.
  - `changed`: new `verified` row with the merged values, `effective_from = started_at`.
  - `pending` / `no_baseline`: a held row (pending_review/rejected) whose seven + two stored values equal the merged values (6 decimals, `None == None`) is re-observed as today; else a new `pending_review` row with the merged values. A held row whose **seven extra columns are all NULL** (held under v2.30.0, before the columns existed) is compared on input and output only, so a v2.30.0 `pending_review` row is not duplicated and a value an admin rejected under v2.30.0 stays rejected instead of coming back as a new `pending_review` row with the extras filled in. Its NULL extras are left as they are (after an approval the next sync fills them as `enriched`).
- `price_sync_runs.changes` counts `"changed"` only; `pending` counts `"pending"` + `"no_baseline"` (unchanged). `"enriched"` appears only in `summary.channels`.

### C10. Payload (`GET /api/pricing`, `backend/pricing_payload.py`)

- `families` order: `PROVIDER_ORDER` (Anthropic, OpenAI, Amazon), then `FAMILY_ORDER`.
- `tiers` always has five keys in this order: `cp`, `openai_list`, `global`, `us` (object or `null`) and `in_region` (list). `SINGLE_TIERS = ("cp", "openai_list", "global", "us")`.
- Every cell object (single tiers and `in_region` elements) gains:

```json
"cache_read": 0.2, "cache_write": 5, "cache_write_1h": 8,
"long": null
```

  `long` is `{"input": n, "output": n, "cache_read": n|null, "cache_write": n|null}` when both `long_input` and `long_output` are set, else `null`. Values use `price_number` (`None` stays `null`). `pending` (when present) gains the same four keys from the pending row.
- Grouping (single-tier best group and `in_region` elements) compares all nine values, verification and the full pending value set.
- `models` (the cost map) excludes `openai_list` channels.
- References: source id `openai-pricing` → `{"kind": "openai_doc", "title_en": "OpenAI API pricing (Standard)", "title_ko": "OpenAI API 요금 (Standard)", "url": OPENAI_REFERENCE_URL}` (as_of = latest observed date, else `SEED_SOURCE_DATE`). Other reference kinds unchanged.
- `families[].notes[]` = the note without `basis_ko`/`basis_en`, always with `source` and `source_id` (`note_source_id(fk)` filled in for manual notes that lack one). A note whose `source` is not `manual_note` has its `source_id` cited through the same footnote numbering (`cite`) right after that family's cells, so it always has a number; a `manual_note` note is **not** cited through `cite` and gets its number from its own `manual_note` reference, appended after the official pages (as today). Every note's `source_id` therefore has exactly one reference.
- Admin `GET /api/admin/pricing/pending`: `PriceValue` gains optional `cache_read`, `cache_write`, `cache_write_1h`, `long_input`, `long_output`, `long_cache_read`, `long_cache_write` (default `None`); `change` carries a ratio for every field set on both sides (input/output ratios stay required when a current row exists).

### C11. Exports (`backend/pricing_export.py`)

- `TIER_KEYS = ("cp", "openai_list", "global", "us", "in_region")`; CSV rows in that order per family.
- `CSV_HEADER = ["provider", "family", "channel", "regions", "model_ids", "input_usd_per_1m", "output_usd_per_1m", "cache_read_usd_per_1m", "cache_write_usd_per_1m", "cache_write_1h_usd_per_1m", "long_input_usd_per_1m", "long_output_usd_per_1m", "long_cache_read_usd_per_1m", "long_cache_write_usd_per_1m", "verification", "observed_at", "footnotes", "source_ids"]`; `None` → empty field; values via `price_text`.
- Markdown columns per provider: anthropic `("cp", "global", "us", "in_region")`, openai `("openai_list", "global", "us", "in_region")`, amazon `("global", "us", "in_region")` (no blank column: each Markdown table stands alone). Header = model label + `TIER_TITLES` below; separator row has as many `---` cells as headers.
- Markdown cell = lines joined by `<br>`: line 1 = today's text (pair, regions, unverified, pending) followed by the footnote refs; line 2 = cache items when any is set; line 3 = long-context items when `long` is set. `in_region` elements are joined by `<br><br>`. Prices via `price_text`.
  - The pending part of line 1 is `(<검토 대기 | Pending review> <items>)` with the screen's `pendingDetail` rule (C12): the pair `new_in / new_out` when input or output differs, then each cache item (`cache_read`, `cache_write`, `cache_write_1h` wording of line 2) whose pending value is set and differs, then the whole long-context line of the pending value (line 3 wording) when `long` differs and the pending `long` is set; items joined by `, `; when nothing differs, the pair. Examples: `4 / 20 (검토 대기 9 / 45, 캐시 읽기 0.9, 긴 컨텍스트 18 / 67.5)`, `4 / 20 (Pending review cache read 0.3)`.
  - KO line 2: `캐시 읽기 0.2, 쓰기 5, 1시간 쓰기 8`; EN: `cache read 0.2, write 5, 1h write 8` (only the fields that are set, in that order).
  - KO line 3: `긴 컨텍스트 20 / 75, 캐시 읽기 2, 쓰기 25`; EN: `long context 20 / 75, cache read 2, write 25`.
- A family note's Markdown item ends with the footnote number of the note's `source_id`.

Third line on GPT rows: long context`. "입력 / 출력", "1M 토큰당 USD", "프롬프트 캐싱", "긴 컨텍스트" (and the EN equivalents) keep today's `whitespace-nowrap font-medium text-gray-300` emphasis.

Items follow `New value` mid-sentence, so in EN every item label starts lower case (`New value cache read $0.50`, `New value $5.50 / $33.00, write $6.875, long context $11.00 / $49.50, cache read $1.10, write $13.75`); KO is unchanged. The Markdown export uses the same item rule (C11).

### C13. Frontend contract

`frontend/src/lib/types.ts`:

```ts
export interface PricingLongContext { input: number; output: number; cache_read: number | null; cache_write: number | null }
export interface PricingPriceFields {
  input: number; output: number;
  cache_read: number | null; cache_write: number | null; cache_write_1h: number | null;
  long: PricingLongContext | null;
}
export interface PricingPending extends PricingPriceFields { id: number; observed_at: string }
export interface PricingTier extends PricingPriceFields {
  model_ids: string[]; source_ids: string[]; footnotes: number[];
  verification: PriceVerification; observed_at: string | null; pending: PricingPending | null;
}
// PricingFamily.tiers: { cp; openai_list: PricingTier | null; global; us; in_region: PricingInRegionTier[] }
// PricingNote: + source: "manual_note" | "openai_doc"; source_id: string   (basis fields are never sent)
// PricingReference.kind: + "openai_doc"
```

`frontend/src/lib/pricingTable.ts`:

```ts
export type PricingTierKey = "cp" | "openai_list" | "global" | "us" | "in_region";
export function tierLabel(key: PricingTierKey, lang: "ko" | "en"): string;          // C12 tier titles
export const PROVIDER_COLUMNS: Record<PricingFamily["provider"], (PricingTierKey | null)[]> = {
  anthropic: ["cp", "global", "us", "in_region"],
  openai: ["openai_list", "global", "us", "in_region"],
  amazon: [null, "global", "us", "in_region"],   // null = the blank first column (header and cells empty)
};
export function headerParts(label: string): string[];   // "AWS Bedrock - Global CRIS" -> ["AWS Bedrock -", "Global CRIS"]; no " - " -> [label]
export function formatUnitPrice(v: number): string;     // 2 to 6 decimals, trailing zeros past 2 trimmed: 4 -> "$4.00", 12.5 -> "$12.50", 1.375 -> "$1.375", 0.0825 -> "$0.0825", 0 -> "$0.00"
export type PriceItem = { label: string; value: string };
export function cacheItems(p: PricingPriceFields, lang: "ko" | "en"): PriceItem[];  // C12 cache items, set fields only
export function longItems(p: PricingPriceFields, lang: "ko" | "en"): PriceItem[];   // [] when long is null
export function pendingDetail(tier: PricingTier, lang: "ko" | "en"): string;       // C12 pending detail
```

`TIER_LABELS` and `noteReferenceId` are removed (every caller moves to `tierLabel` / `note.source_id`). `formatPricePair`, `textRuns`, `utcDate`, `notesForTier`, `tierBadges`, `costFromPrices` keep their signatures (`tierBadges` uses `pendingDetail` and `note.source_id`).

`PricingPanel.tsx`: each provider table renders `PROVIDER_COLUMNS[provider]`; a `null` column is `<td aria-hidden="true" data-tier="none">` in the header row and in every body row (no text, no "—", same `<col>` width, so the three tables keep identical column positions). Header text renders `headerParts(tierLabel(key, lang))`, each part `whitespace-nowrap`, separated by a space. A price entry renders, in order: the price line (unchanged), `data-cache-line` (when `cacheItems` is non-empty), `data-long-line` (when `longItems` is non-empty), model ids (toggle), badges. Cache/long lines: `mt-0.5 block text-[11px] leading-snug text-gray-400`, items joined by `, `, each item `whitespace-nowrap` except the long-context pair item, whose label and value are two `whitespace-nowrap` spans separated by a space.
